It's empty though and still worked — probably fell back). To be safe and deterministic, use `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc`.\n\nWait — but the Suricata config comment says \"The extension .mgc is added to the value here.\" That means Suricata/libmagic appends .mgc. If I set `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc`, libmagic would look for `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc.mgc`? Let's check: magic_load(ctx, filename) — libmagic's magic_load with a filename tries the file as-is; if it's a compiled database it loads it. Actually libmagic's `apprentice_load` checks: for each magic file in the colon-separated list, it tries `<file>.mgc` first, then `<file>`. So giving `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc` → tries `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc.mgc` (fails) then `/usr/share/misc/magic.mgc` (succeeds, it IS a compiled db). Both paths work, as the `file -m` test just confirmed — `file -m /usr/share/misc/magic.mgc` succeeded.\n\nAnd Suricata's own fopen check requires the literal path to be openable — `.mgc` is a regular file → passes cleanly. The bare `magic` path is a directory; fopen(dir,\"r\") succeeds on glibc but that's fragile/unintended.\n\nDecision: `magic-file: /usr/share/misc/magic.mgc`. Verified loadable.
